## Epic X7: Test Suite De-stubbing

*Fifty-four test files declare the module they are meant to be testing instead of importing it. The
reason that was tolerated no longer exists.*

> **Why now:** the stub pattern existed because the linker gap made cross-module imports fail. That gap
> closed upstream — `UPSTREAM.md:77` records the verification. The excuse is dead; the stubs are not.
>
> **Unblocked 2026-09-20.** The toolchain is available, so execution queues behind the green build
> rather than upstream: a de-stubbed test has to compile to be worth anything. X7.1 needs nothing and is
> overdue.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| X7.1 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23, inventory committed to `docs/test-coverage.md` | **Inventory and triage the 95** — Re-measured 2026-09-23. This row said 54 of 86. Discovery finds **121** test files (88 under `tests/` plus **33 under `packages/moke/tests/`**), so both the numerator and the denominator were stale. **62 of 88** under `tests/` import no production module, and **33 of 33** moke tests import nothing at all — so 27% of the "122 passing" suite compiles against nothing and cannot fail. Only **26** of 121 import any production module, reaching **21** distinct `core.*` modules. Triage each: de-stub / delete / keep-with-reason. Two traps: `tests/unit/privacy/test_patterns.tk` is a genuinely good test whose asserts are wrapped in `matches`/`rejects` helpers at lines 21/25, so a raw `assert(` count flags it wrongly; and moke's 33 belong to the moke onboarding wave, not after it — a test that compiles against nothing tells you nothing about whether moke built<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-23.** Re-measured a third time and the denominator moved again: discovery finds **135** test files, not 121 — other stories added tests through the day. The inventory is in [test-coverage.md](test-coverage.md) under **X7.1 — the inventory**, with a per-directory census, the list of production modules actually reached, and the files that still reach none. Both traps named in this row were real and are recorded there, plus a third: a test can be incapable of failing for a reason unrelated to stubs — `tests/unit/storage/test_settings.tk` had eleven assertions written as bare statements with the returned bool discarded, an unconditional `pass=pass+1`, and `fail` declared, never incremented, and returned. The triage also settled moke: its 33 are **untestable**, not lazy — `build/trees/moke/break-report.txt` carries 52 `COMPILE_FAIL` lines over 42 source files and every module those tests are named after is in it, while the nineteen page handlers that do compile export only `get(i64):i64` and take an ooke request handle no test can construct. 21 of the 33 make no `t.assert` call at all. |
| X7.2 | XL | **Partial** — 2026-09-23; all of `tests/` de-stubbed, moke and CLI blocked | **De-stub the tests that guard a privacy or governance path first** — Priority order by what a passing stub currently misrepresents: the privacy pipeline, the governance gateway, the placeholder store, the router, then everything else. A test that re-declares its subject cannot fail when the subject breaks, which is how A13 came to be marked complete while the browser handler posted raw text.<br><br>**Status detail — 2026-09-23.** Worked in the priority order this row names. Every stubbed file under `tests/` is de-stubbed: the suite went from 37 test files importing production code to **100 of 135**, and from 22 distinct production modules exercised to **80**. **2423** assertions now run, the great majority of them against real modules. Directories finished: `storage`, `governance`, `privacy`, `memory`, `mcp`, `agents`, `optimiser`, `providers`, `models`, `router`, `shared`, `browser`, `integration`, `policy` and the two files at the root of `tests/`. **What is NOT done, and cannot be here:** `packages/moke/tests/` — all 33 — because 42 moke source files do not compile (`build/trees/moke/break-report.txt`, 52 `COMPILE_FAIL` lines) and every module those tests are named after is among them; and `tests/unit/cli/` — both files — because `scripts/run_tests.sh` passes a single `-I build/interfaces` and links `packages/browser/build/ooke` alone, so the CLI tree is unreachable from a test although it builds clean. The de-stubbing found **37** production defects, listed in [test-coverage.md](test-coverage.md). **Four were fixed by other workers the same day** — `governance.scorecard`'s COUNT aggregate, `optimiser.llmlingua`'s four inverted matches, `optimiser.profiler`'s findmin/findmax, and `router.intent`'s classification — each with a comment citing the measurement. Nine tests are RED, every one of them on a measured defect and none on a test-authoring fault; the suite is **126 pass / 9 fail / 135**. |
| X7.3 | M | **Partial** — 2026-09-23, live on 15 of the 17 test directories | **Make a stubbed test a gate failure** — Add the stub-marker check to `scripts/quality-gate.sh` with `scripts/known-defects.txt` as the shrinking allowlist, so the count can only go down.<br><br>**Status detail — 2026-09-23.** [`scripts/check_test_stubs.py`](../scripts/check_test_stubs.py) is wired into the static-checks step of [`quality-gate.sh`](../scripts/quality-gate.sh). It is enforced **per directory**, not repo-wide, and that is the point: switching it on globally only after X7.2 finishes would leave it unenforced for exactly the period in which the count could grow back. A directory joins the script's `ENFORCED` list the moment it reaches zero and never leaves; `tests/unit/storage/` was first. Three things fail a file — no live non-`std.` import, a commented-out import (the stub pattern's own signature), or a stub **declaration** marker; the marker patterns match declaration forms only and never the word in prose, because a de-stubbed test that explains what its stubs used to be must not be flagged for saying so. Proven able to fail: pointed at `tests/unit/governance/` before X7.2 ran there, it named twelve of that directory's fourteen test files and gave a reason for each. As of the end of the session **fifteen of the seventeen directories that hold test files are enforced** — all but `packages/moke/tests/` and `tests/unit/cli/`, each blocked for a reason stated in test-coverage.md. Two guards keep the list honest: a directory named in `ENFORCED` that holds no test files fails rather than passing vacuously, and the marker rule is judged at comment nesting depth 1 only, so a de-stubbed test that QUOTES the stub it removed is not flagged for doing so — reading the raw text flagged four such files before that was fixed. `scripts/known-defects.txt` is the allowlist and **carries no entries for this check**, which is the intended state — every enforced directory reached zero before enforcement began. `**Partial**` until the remaining directories are added. |
| X7.4 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **Restate coverage once** — Re-measure and update `docs/test-coverage.md` and the claims register with both numbers (files carrying a test, modules actually exercised) and what each means.<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-23.** [test-coverage.md](test-coverage.md) restated against the corrected denominators, keeping the two-figure discipline: **135** test files discovered (the 86 figure counted `tests/` only and missed moke's 33), **100** of them importing production code, **80** distinct production modules exercised — so **20%** of 408 modules are actually exercised against **35.5%** that merely have a test file named after them. A **third** figure is added and is the smallest: **31** of those 80 modules have no importer anywhere in `packages/**` or `src/**`, so only **49 (12%)** are both exercised and reachable in the product. Two of the 31 are worth naming — `core.privacy.placeholderstore`, which holds the X8.2 field-level sealing of the placeholder mapping, and `core.storage.ephemeral`, whose own test says it covers the property the privacy pipeline depends on. The "has a test file" figure did not move at all while the exercised figure trebled, which is the argument for reporting both. Two stale tables were deleted rather than updated — `All Test Files` and `Test files that import production code` — because a hand-maintained list of test files in a tree this active is wrong the day it is written, and both were; the census that replaces them is generated from the import graph. The claims register is NOT touched here: `docs/claims.md` belongs to another story and to another worker. |
| X7.5 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **`test_regex_detector.tk` tests nothing about the detector** — Found 2026-09-23. Every assertion is `str.len` or `str.contains` over a **literal** — e.g. `str.len("123456789")==9` — and the file imports neither `core.privacy.regex` nor `core.privacy.patterns`. It passes, and it always will, whatever the detector does. Replace it with assertions against the real module, or delete it — `tests/unit/privacy/test_regex_spans.tk` now exercises the detector properly and is the model<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-23.** Rewritten rather than deleted, because `test_regex_spans.tk` covers the spans and the PL1.10 boundary rejection but leaves most of what this file originally claimed uncovered. It now imports `core.privacy.regex` and asserts the pattern inventory itself — ten named patterns, each with a non-empty expression and the sensitivity its type warrants, because a pattern silently dropped from that list fails nothing else in the suite and a prompt that used to be redacted goes out in the clear — plus detection of the types `test_regex_spans.tk` never touches (credit_card, ip_v4, ssn_us, au_abn, url), the negative cases that decide whether the detector cries wolf, and `detectwithpatterns` against a restricted subset. The file is quoted at the head of [test-coverage.md](test-coverage.md) as the epic's problem statement in miniature. |
| X7.6 | S | | **The privacy harness declares expected entity types and throws them away** — Found 2026-09-23 (AD1.8). `core.privacy.testharness`'s `$testcase` carries `expectpiitypes` for all seven cases and `runtestcase` never asserts it: only the count, the sensitivity and guardian presence are checked. And it would **fail on arrival** if added, because two cases expect `"phone"` while the detector emits `"phone_au"`. That gap is why the phone case passed its count assertion at both 2 and 3 entities — `>=` against `expectentities` — and the over-detection was caught only by the sensitivity assertion. Assert the types, and settle the naming |

## Epic LC1: Local Compute Validation on Target Hardware

*Nine stories are marked Done for behaviour that has never executed on the hardware it was written for.*

> **This epic is blocked on a purchase, not on the toolchain.** `README.md:354-360`, `docs/claims.md` §J
> and `docs/features-loke.md:161` all record the same blocker: there is no Apple Silicon test machine.
> VM1.4's containerised harness is a partial substitute and cannot validate unified memory or MLX.
> See decision **H** in the open-decisions register.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| LC1.1 | S | Blocked — hardware | **Acquire or arrange target hardware** — A base Apple Silicon Mac Mini is sufficient and is the recommendation. A cloud GPU instance cannot substitute: unified memory is the premise of the layer-offload design and a discrete-GPU instance has a PCIe boundary that predicts nothing about a Mini; MLX is Apple-Silicon-only, so a non-Apple host validates it not at all. `benchmarks/README.md:42` requires every local-inference figure to state which host produced it. |
| LC1.2 | L | Blocked — LC1.1 | **Run the five unverifiable F2 claims** — `docs/claims.md` J1-J5. Record each through `benchmarks/lib/result.py` with the host, model version, workload and N, so the results are admissible rather than anecdotal. Any that fails becomes its own defect. |
| LC1.3 | M | Blocked — LC1.1 | **MLX: exercise or withdraw** — The MLX path has zero importers and `generate()` would return `""` if called; F2.9 has no implementing code at all despite being marked Done. Either make it work on the target hardware or remove the MLX claim from the public site and the feature documents. |
| LC1.4 | M | Blocked — LC1.1 | **Validate the CPS 230 continuity control** — `docs/specifications/policy-examples/apra-cps-230.yaml:82,213` carries `validation_status: UNVALIDATED` on the local-provider fallback and states twice that an untested fallback must not be presented to an assessor as a continuity control. This is the control the whole preset rests on. Exercise the degraded path end to end with the network disabled, then change that field and nothing before. |
| LC1.5 | L | Blocked — LC1.1 | **Memory-splitting and layer offload** — Measure what actually fits and at what throughput when a model exceeds available memory, on real unified memory. This is the technique the tiered-inference design assumes; it has never been measured. Report tokens/second and resident memory per tier, and state the physical-security difference between a desk machine and a remote host in any write-up. |

## Epic LS1: Canonical Form Sweep

*toke publishes a measured pattern catalogue and a linter that enforces it. loke has never been run
through either.*

> **Why this is not bikeshedding.** `docs/guide/11-patterns-and-efficiency.md` in the toke repository
> picks one form for each everyday construct **by measurement** — the form that costs the fewest tokens
> under the decision tokenizer *and* runs as fast in as little memory as any alternative. For a project
> whose premise is reducing what is spent on a model, the canonical form is the product, not a style
> preference.
>
> Measured across 223 loke source files on 2026-09-20: **1,668 violations**.
>
> | Rule | Count | Files | Category |
> |---|---|---|---|
> **Re-measured 2026-09-23 over all 535 non-archived `.tk`.** The 2026-09-20 figures below were
> against 223 files and are stale in both directions; LS1.2–LS1.4 must be sized against these.
> The split matters because the trees outside the build cannot be gated until they compile:
>
> | rule | **built set** (`loke`) | repo-wide | category |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | `single-use-let` | 854 | **1,823** | hint |
> | `string-concat-chain` | 316 | **814** | warning |
> | `unused-import` | 196 | **306** | warning |
> | **`discarded-value-result`** | **19** | **188** | **error** |
> | `mutable-never-mutated` | 0 | 41 | warning |
> | `unused-let` / `loop-rebuilds-array` / `mut-flag-if` / `flag-soup` | 26 | 58 | mixed |
> | **total** | **1,411** | **3,230** | |
>
> Where the repo-wide error count lives: moke 87, legacy `src/` 79, built set 19, cli 3.
> `src/platform/i18n/locales.tk` alone carries **62** of the 188, in 94 lines — a file nobody has
> ever run. This is why LS1.5's lint gate must be per-tree: a repo-wide error gate would go red the
> instant moke's census is baselined and stay red through the whole onboarding.
>
> **Token efficiency here means canonical constructs, not minified source.** `toke --fmt` is the
> canonical *source* form and makes files slightly larger; `--min` is the shipped form. Do not minify
> source to chase a byte count — the catalogue's verdicts are what count.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| LS1.1 | S |  | **The 30 `discarded-value-result`, first and on their own** — This is a defect story, not hygiene. The rule is **error** category and its rationale is blunt: *"the discarded value is a bug in every case"*. Arrays and maps have value semantics, so a bare `arr.push(v);` or `trace.steps.push(...)` statement **returns a new collection and throws it away**, and the syntax card notes a bare push *can crash at runtime*. 30 sites in 11 files, worst `agents/observability.tk:222`. The program does not do what it says it does; fix before anything cosmetic. |
| LS1.1a | L | **Done** | **Thirteen accumulations that silently do nothing** — The most consequential defect found in the whole migration, and **no lint rule reports it**. Each of these reads as an append or an increment and is a no-op `==` comparison in statement position:<br><br>**`privacy/placeholder.tk:41`** — `map.entries==str.arraypush(map.entries;entry)`. **The placeholder map never stores an entry.** Everything downstream of it — restoration, the mapping shown in the privacy review, PL1's collision work — rests on a table that is always empty.<br>**`governance/policy.tk:85,87`** — `controls==@(controls;"human-visible-preview";"cost-confirmation";…)`. **The controls a policy decision requires are never attached to it**, so a decision that should demand preview and cost confirmation demands nothing.<br>**`agents/registry.tk:42,188`** — `reg.agents==@(reg.agents;agent)` and `reg.runs==@(reg.runs;run)`. **Registering an agent never adds it to the registry**, and no run is ever recorded.<br>**`agents/sandbox.tk:139,158` and `agents/executor.tk:41,77`** — `sandbox.violations==@(sandbox.violations;msg)`. **No sandbox or executor violation is ever recorded.** With `recordcost` (also a no-op, fixed) that means the sandbox tracked neither cost nor violations — the two things it exists to track.<br>**`governance/use_cases.tk:126`** — use cases are never registered.<br>**`mcp/discovery.tk:122`** — discovered MCP servers are never added to the list.<br>**`memory/search.tk:93`** — keyword relevance never accumulates, so multi-keyword search ranks by insertion order.<br>**`moke/src/dashboard_flow.tk:167`** — appends a computed value into a card label; this one failing is a mercy, since that is the display-channel smuggling NC1.5 strips.<br><br>**Root cause.** All thirteen try to mutate a struct reached through a parameter. toke has value semantics: a callee cannot write to its caller's struct, so these were wrong before the `=`/`==` migration and it only changed how they fail. The fix per site is for the function to return the updated value and every caller to use it — not a one-character edit.<br><br>**What this means for the backlog.** Several of these sit under stories marked **Done**: the agent registry and sandbox (F7/GA-series), policy control attachment (GA1.8), MCP discovery, and the placeholder store. They belong in **RV1** as reopen candidates, and the placeholder one should be checked before any PL1 measurement is taken, because a recall figure computed against an always-empty map measures nothing.<br><br>**How to find them.** `([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*)\s*==\s*(.*\1.*)` anchored to statement position. Note BSD `grep -E` silently matches nothing for a backreference, so use Python; my first sweep with grep reported zero and I believed it<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done in the built set; 11 more found in moke 2026-09-23** — 11 of 13 landed across `0b1f60e`, `eb6f166`, `67ca50e`. The F10.17a sweep then found **11 further sites, all in `packages/moke`**, which is not in the build: `ml/engine.tk:168,178` — the k-means centroid update is `sum.get(di2)==sum.get(di2) + …`, so centroid computation does nothing; `dashboard_flow.tk:150,167,224`; `ddl.tk:266,277,288,299`; `pages/api/agents.tk:86,121` — a discarded call result. They land with the moke onboarding wave. All are instances of upstream **T-26**: the compiler's own advice for a field assignment is `==` |
| LS1.1b | M | **Done** | **`x=@(x;item)` builds a nested pair, it does not append** — Verified by running: `@(a;4)` where `a` has three elements produces an array of length **2**, while `a.push(4)` produces length 4. So `@(arr;item)` is an array **literal** containing the array and the item.<br><br>135 sites across 32 files used it as an append: agent registries and run lists, overnight job queues, pipeline step results, quota rules, policy lists, CLI column widths, discovered servers. Every one built `[[[…], item]` instead of a growing list, so each accumulation produced a malformed nested structure rather than the collection its caller expected.<br><br>This is also why so many type errors read **"expected 'array', got 'array'"** — the two arrays differ in element type because one of them is nesting.<br><br>All 135 are converted. 117 went mechanically to `.push()`; the remaining 18 were multi-item literals and struct-field targets that needed the owning function to return the updated value — `agents/registry.tk`'s `register`/`recordrun` and `optimiser/profiler.tk`'s `extractcolvalues` among them.<br><br>Same root cause as LS1.1a: value semantics. `@(…)` constructs, `.push()` appends, and neither mutates the receiver |
| LS1.1c | L | **Done** | **52 bindings that shadowed instead of assigning, across 18 files** — `let x=…` inside an `if` or a loop body declares a *new* binding; it does not assign to an `x` from the enclosing scope. So a line that reads exactly like an accumulation or an increment does nothing, and toke also emits invalid LLVM IR for it (T-6), which is how this was finally found: the link failed with `Instruction does not dominate all uses!` and no source location.<br><br>What was broken, all of it compiling:<br><br>**`memory/privacy.tk:shouldstore`** — `let blocked=true` in the branch, so this returned `true` for every input. **A user's opt-out topics were never honoured and content they asked never to be stored was stored.** P0 by this project's own standard.<br>**`models/queue.tk`** — all four state transitions (`markrunning`, `markcompleted`, `markfailed`, `cancel`) were **infinite loops**: `let lv=1` was meant as a break and `let i=i+1` as an increment, and neither assigned. Each also wrote through `.set(i;job)` and discarded the result, and returned `void`, so a terminating version would still have changed nothing.<br>**`models/registry.tk`** — `findfortask` always returned empty, `findbest` and `byid` always errored, and `register` had an **empty body**. Model selection could never succeed. `findbest` also took the first acceptable candidate rather than the best; it now keeps the highest quality score above the tier's speed floor.<br>**`models/ollama.tk`** — `listmodels` and `listrunning` always returned an empty list, so loke never saw a single installed model.<br>**`memory/shorthand.tk`** — `expand` and `expanddotchains` returned their input unchanged; no shorthand was ever expanded.<br>**`memory/mining.tk`** — all three counters stayed 0 and `mineall` collected nothing.<br>**`memory/mcp_server.tk`** — `handlesearchresults` and `handlefactsresults` were infinite loops returning `[]`.<br>**`memory/aaak.tk:encodetext`** — returned an empty document for every input.<br>**`memory/search.tk`** — `searchbuildsingleresult` took its accumulator as an `i64`, appended into a branch-local binding and returned `void`: **every memory search returned no results** however many drawers matched.<br>**`privacy/test_harness.tk`** — `runtestcase` threw away every assertion, and `formatresults` was an infinite loop. This is why AD1.8 found it excluded from `run_tests.sh`.<br>**`optimiser/profiler.tk:infercoltype`** — `allint==false` in a match arm is a discarded comparison, so every column typed as int.<br>**`optimiser/budget.tk:resetperiod`** — `manager.usage.get(i)==$budgetusage{…}` compared and discarded; no usage row was ever reset.<br>**`governance/policy.tk:classifyrisk`** — `basetier==$medium` discarded, so **a confidential request with a low use-case risk was never escalated.**<br>**`storage/dashboard.tk`** — widget preferences never persisted.<br>**`storage/ephemeral.tk:put`** — `json.set` returns a new object and the result was discarded, so **the ephemeral placeholder map stored nothing** and response restoration could never restore.<br>**`cli/proxy.tk:parseopts`** — every option returned its default and the loop never terminated.<br>**`cli/session.tk`**, **`cli/proxy_config.tk`**, **`browser/pages/installer.tk`** (always reported Ollama as not installed), **`browser/src/components/table.tk`** (JS `dir = 'ascending'` assigned rather than compared, so the sort direction was always ascending).<br><br>**Held by `scripts/find_shadowed_lets.py`**, scope-aware — two sequential loops each declaring their own `lv` are disjoint siblings and legal; the defect is specifically an inner declaration inside an outer one's braces. In the gate. |
| LS1.1d | S | **Done** | **A missing semicolon before `<` turns a return into a comparison** — `<` is both the return operator and less-than, so a statement followed by `<$ok(1)` with no `;` between them parses as one comparison whose value is discarded; the function returns the comparison. One site (`feedback/reporter.tk:submit`, which therefore never reported a successful submission). Only ever *reported* when the left operand is a void call, and then as `void type only allowed for function results` from clang. Held by `scripts/find_missing_return_semi.py` in the gate. |
| LS1.1e | M | **Done** | **Seven toke defects that compile and fail at link, each with a minimal reproduction** — filed as [`docs/upstream/toke-issues.md`](upstream/toke-issues.md) T-1 to T-7, with the loke-side workaround for each: `bool!$err` and `void!$err` are both unusable and become `i64!$err` (17 declarations converted); a `mt` of 3+ arms needs its subject's sum type visible, by annotation or by lifting the match into a typed function (17 sites); an `i32` array index is widened with `zext i1`, so indices are `i64`; a bare type name in expression position and an unknown method call both get **no diagnostic at all** and surface only as `use of undefined value` or `Undefined symbols` — each hid a whole non-functional path (`shared/config.tk` had **never read loke's config file**, calling a fluent TOML API that does not exist; `policy/feedback.tk` passed SQL where a store handle belongs, which happened to match the arity). Four sweeps plus an IR check hold all seven, and the document asks upstream for a source location on codegen failures, which would have saved most of this. |
| LS1.1f | L | **Done** | **104 functions ended in a bare `mt` and returned garbage instead of its value** — E4071 catches a trailing bare match when the function returns a scalar. It does not when the function returns a sum or a struct: that compiles, and the function returns whatever was in the return slot. `db.exec`, `db.query`, `db.queryone`, `settings.getstr`, every `sync_queue` operation, `dispatcher.dispatch` and `governance.policy.evaluate` were all in that hole — each computed the right answer and discarded it, and `policy.evaluate`'s caller segfaulted because the value it matched on was never a `$policydecision`. 94 fixed; the other 10 were cases where the match is the first statement rather than the only one, and E5002 caught the over-application. Upstream T-19. |
| LS1.1g | L | **Done** | **60 loops broke one iteration too late** — `lp(...){ if(i>count){br} … }` reads `arr.get(count)`, one past the end. For an array of structs that is a garbage pointer and dereferencing a field of it is a segfault; for an array of strings it silently processes one extra element. Every privacy layer had at least one: `regex.tk`, `presidio.tk`, `placeholder.tk`, `pipeline.tk` (nine). Held by `scripts/find_offbyone_loops.py`. |
| LS1.1h | M | **Partial** | **An `f64` returned across a loke module boundary is corrupted** — upstream T-20. Was sized XL across 23 functions. Measured against toke `c4e4704`: **only one live corrupting call site existed in the built tree** — `shared.types.f64abs` called from `eval/compare.tk:56,57`, which reported a score delta and a cost delta that were neither of them the measured value. Fixed by `t.f64absfixed`, which does the abs and the formatting in one call so a **str** crosses instead of an f64. The other 22 are same-module or have no caller, because their consumers are unwired; they become live the moment GA1.5–GA1.7 wire them, so each needs `f64hundredths` or an equivalent before its consumer lands — that is the remaining work, and it is M, not XL. **`json.getfloat` is exonerated**: a stdlib builtin is a `tk_*_w` wrapper that really does carry the bit pattern in an i64 and really is bitcast back, so both halves of that ABI agree and arm C passes. The separate ~62-site defect where a `$row` is read with a json accessor is **LS1.1i**, a different bug. Mechanism, for upstream: `llvm.c:1639` states the intent — *"Cross-module ABI: all non-void returns use i64 (pointers via ptrtoint, bools via zext, floats via bitcast)"* — and line 1641 passes a literal `"i64"`, discarding the correct `"double"` line 1629 computed; but the definition side (`resolve_llvm_type`, `llvm.c:680`) emits a real `double`, and neither side performs the promised bitcast. The callee returns in `xmm0`, the caller reads `rax`. The parameter is `double` on both sides, which is exactly why parameters cross intact<br><br>**Status detail:** **Re-sized 2026-09-23 by measurement, not by re-reading.** `tests/unit/shared/test_f64_boundary.tk` asserts all four arms; arm B is a tripwire that fails the day T-20 is fixed *Scope narrowed again 2026-09-23 by measurement: an `f64` **field of a struct** returned across a module boundary is **intact** — arms E and F of `tests/unit/shared/test_f64_boundary.tk`. Asked because `core.metrics.collector.summarise` returns a `$metricssummary` with four f64 fields that `loke stats` prints, so if they were corrupted every figure that command shows would be wrong. They are not. A struct crosses as a pointer, so the field is loaded from memory rather than read out of a return register — which is the same reason parameters are unaffected. **So this story covers bare f64 returns only**, and no struct needs migrating on T-20's account.* |
| LS1.1i | M | **Done** | **~62 sites read a `$row` with a `json.*` accessor and get nothing** — a row handle is not a JSON object, so `json.str`/`json.i64`/`json.getfloat` over a `db.many` row return empty or zero. Not an f64 problem: `json.getfloat` itself is fine (LS1.1h arm C). `audit.tk:38-42` already documents the bug and the fix, and `storage/db.tk` already has `rowstr`/`rowi64`/`rowf64hundredths`. The proof is `governance/quota.tk:90-95`, where one function reads five columns, four correctly and `limit_cost_usd` through `json.getfloat`. Worst affected: `memory/graph.tk:91-112`, where `entityfromrow`/`relationfromrow` read **every** field through `json.*`, so every field of every memory-graph entity and relation is empty or zero; and `storage/settings.tk:81`, so `settings.getfloat` returns `0.0` for every float setting. 16 files: `memory/graph.tk` 12, `governance/dsar.tk` 12, `storage/sync_queue.tk` 11, `memory/privacy.tk` 4, `privacy/placeholder_store.tk` 3, `memory/palace.tk` 3, `memory/export.tk` 3, `governance/quota.tk` 3, `storage/settings.tk` 2, `governance/report.tk` 2, `governance/monitoring.tk` 2, and one each in `storage/dashboard.tk`, `metrics/collector.tk`, `memory/decay.tk`, `governance/rules_engine.tk`, `feedback/store.tk`. Larger, more certain and cheaper than LS1.1h, and it had no story ID until now<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done — 2026-09-23.** 78 sites in 18 files, not the 62 estimated: `memory/palace.tk` had 8 not 3 (the row variable is `rows.get(ii)`, never `row`) and `feedback/learning.tk` had 3 not 1 (two were `json.geti32`). Nine function signatures were also lying about their row type and are fixed. Every column name checked against its DDL; every float column goes through `rowf64hundredths` with the division done locally after the crossing. Sites reading genuine parsed JSON were correctly left alone. **Follow-ons filed separately:** M1.7, GA5.10, LS1.1k, and 14 more sites of the same bug in `report-engine.tk` and `governance/dashboard.tk` |
| LS1.1j | L |  | **Convert the four `!$lokeerr` chokepoints to sentinel + predicate** — `dispatcher.dispatch`/`dispatchwithguardian`, `selector.select`, `router.route`, `gateway.submit`. T-18 makes every one of their error arms dead, so a failure reads as success. That is why GA1.5–GA1.7 are held: a failed `selector.select` read as success picks a garbage provider, and for sensitivity-scored routing that can mean sending restricted data to a cloud endpoint — introduced by the story meant to prevent it. Follow the two patterns already landed: `storage/settings.getstror`/`getintor`/`getboolor`, which apply the default inside the producing module on a plain `if`, and `mcp/discovery.probeport` + `isserver()`, which returns a sentinel struct with an empty `name`. One story so the idiom is uniform. **Hard prerequisite for GA1.5, GA1.6, GA1.7** |
| LS1.5a | S |  | **Re-baseline LS1.1–LS1.4 against the measured counts** — done in the preamble above; this row is the reminder that LS1.2's "407", LS1.3's "232" and LS1.4's "999" are the old numbers and must be restated per tree before they are worked |
| LS1.1k | S |  | **The inverse of LS1.1i: a JSON object read through a row accessor** — `memory/export.tk:214-221` (`importjson`) reads elements of a `json.parsearray` result with `db.rowstr(item;...)`. It compiles because `std.db` declares `db.rowstr` as `(i64,i64)->i64` with `"gen":"abi"` — completely untyped — so nothing stops a JSON handle being passed to a row accessor. So memory **import** is as broken as memory **read** was, by the mirror-image mistake, and the two erased signatures on either side are why neither was caught. Use the `json.*` accessors here, and note in `docs/upstream/toke-issues.md` that a `gen:"abi"` signature defeats every call-site check — it is the same root cause as T-23 and T-24 |
| LS1.1l | M | **Done** | *Done 2026-09-23.* **Eight wrappers in `shared.types` returned nothing for as long as they existed** — Found by the LS1.1j worker. Each body was a bare expression with **no `<`**, so toke evaluated it and discarded the result: `strlen("abcdef")` was **0**, `streq("a";"a")` **false**, `strconcat("ab";"cd")` **""**, and `strcontains`, `strlower`, `strslice`, `i32tostr`, `f64tostr` all likewise. `strisempty`, five lines away in the same file, has its `<` and always worked — which is exactly why a skim found nothing. **It was not decorative:** `core/router/escalation.tk` was **entirely dead** (its ladder never matched, and `strlen(x) < 100` was always true because strlen returned 0), `eval/advisor.tk` fell to its default on all four branches, `eval/bench.tk` scored every case 0, `latency_router.tk` logged an empty string — fifteen call sites in five modules, none doing what they read as doing. A **new instance of the T-19 class**: a bare *expression* tail rather than a bare `mt`, which no sweep was looking for. Fixed, with `tests/unit/shared/test_types_wrappers.tk` (13 assertions) holding it, and `scripts/find_discarded_tail_expr.py` now in the gate so it cannot return |
| LS1.1m | M | | **Twelve more functions declare a return type and never return** — Found 2026-09-23 by the new sweep. None has a live external caller, which is the only reason these are debt rather than defects being shipped; they are allowlisted in `scripts/known-defects.txt` against this story. Each needs **reading**, not a mechanical `<`: several are procedure-shaped functions declared `:i64` whose value no caller wants, and the honest fix there is the signature rather than the return. Sites: `browser/pages/installer.tk`, `cli/src/installer.tk`, `core/auth/oauth.tk` (`revoke`), `core/auth/token_store.tk` (three, `savetokens`-shaped), `core/installer/uninstall.tk`, `core/memory/aaak.tk` (`decode`), `core/memory/shorthand.tk` (`preview`), `core/optimiser/cache.tk` (`formatstats` — see GA1.6), `moke/src/token_optimisation.tk` (two). Note the sweep is deliberately narrow: it flags only a function containing **no `<` at all**, because a "last line is a bare expression" heuristic produced **309 hits, almost all false** — the tail was usually the final arm of a multi-line `mt` or the final field of a struct literal, both already returned by a `<` further up |
| LS1.1n | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **`mt` binds a result's arms by POSITION, so 53 matches were inverted** — upstream **T-31**, the general form of T-18 and worse than it: not "the error path is dead" but "success and failure are swapped". Measured at `0b0bc4e`: a plain sum matches correctly by label even when reordered; a STDLIB result reads its discriminant and uses it as an INDEX, so `ok` runs arm 0 and `err` runs arm 1 whatever the arms are called; a result with a user-declared error side never reads the discriminant at all. T-18's text said *"stdlib results are unaffected"* — false, and false because the original probe wrote `$ok` first, the one arrangement in which positional and label binding agree. **53 sites in 20 modules.** `mcp-toke/src/tools.tk` and `core/mcp/client.tk` (16 sites) answered "Parse error" to every well-formed MCP request and logged "failed to listen" without accepting a connection — the whole MCP server unreachable, with no diagnostic anywhere. `privacy/presidio.isavailable` reported a HEALTHY Presidio as unavailable, so the PII layer never ran; `optimiser/llmlingua.isavailable` did the mirror image and returned TRUE for an ABSENT sidecar, a **fail-open** on a privacy-adjacent layer. That pair is why this is gated rather than remembered: the identical misuse of the identical construct, fail-closed in one module and fail-open in the other, invisible to any test because the arms type-check either way. `scripts/find_err_arm_first.py` fails the gate on any result match whose `$ok` arm is not first; `scripts/fix_err_arm_first.py` reorders them, moving arm contents between slots and leaving each slot's whitespace alone so every hunk reads as a reordering rather than a rewrite. Both blank strings and comments length-preservingly — deleting them drifted the reported line numbers past the first comment and named one site 17 lines from its `mt`. |
| LS1.1o | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **`std.json` cannot read a nested value, and both hosted providers depended on it** — upstream **T-32**. The typed API is eight functions and every accessor takes a `Json` plus a FLAT key; there is no `json.obj(Json,str)`. All four routes measured: `json.str` on an object returns `$jsonerr`; a dotted path returns `$jsonerr`; `json.getval` then `json.i64` **SEGFAULTS**; `str.matchbracket` returns `$bracketerr`. The segfault is the instructive one — `getval` is one of ~50 names declared `(i64,i64)->i64` that reach a different representation through `json.parse` (T-17), and handing one the typed `Json` compiles and links because a one-field struct and a handle share an ABI (T-23). So the interface advertises ~60 functions of which 8 are usable, and a wrong pick is a segfault rather than a diagnostic. Consequence: both providers read token usage as `json.str(obj;"usage")`, so every audit row for a hosted request carried `tokens_in = 0`, `tokens_out = 0`, `cost_usd = 0` — in a table that is append-only and hash-chained over those three columns, so the zeros are permanent once written and the chain attests to them. OpenAI additionally returned EMPTY CONTENT for every completion, because `choices[0].message.content` is one level deeper than Anthropic's `content[0].text`. `packages/shared/src/jsonobj.tk` works around it on two things that do work: `Json` is declared `{raw:str}` and `o.raw` holds the whole document, and `$json{raw:…}` can be constructed in loke code and read correctly by the typed accessors. It brace-matches the object out of the raw text, tracking string state and backslash escapes, because a `}` inside a string value is data a provider really sends. `json.arr` IS typed and works, so array descent needs no trick. 16 assertions in `tests/unit/shared/test_jsonobj.tk`. |
| LS1.1p | S | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **Every numeric and boolean `std.json` accessor reports an error for a FALSY value** — upstream **T-33**. Measured with the field present: `json.i64` on `0`, `json.u64` on `0`, `json.f64` on `0.0` and `json.bool` on `false` all return `$jsonerr`; `json.str` on the empty string returns `$ok`, and it survives only because an empty string is still a non-null pointer. So a JSON zero cannot be distinguished from a missing key, and every error arm in a program is doing double duty as "the value was falsy". Fixed in loke rather than only reported: `jx.fieldiszero` asks the document text, exactly rather than heuristically — JSON forbids leading zeros in numbers, so a value whose first character is `0` is `0` or `0.0`, and a field that is present, not falsy and still errored is a genuine type mismatch that gets the caller's fallback. **This is probably T-18, T-31 and T-33 as one defect.** Eleven measurements are consistent with `X!E` being a single machine word carrying the value, with zero standing for the error and no tag at all — which predicts the falsy failure, positional arm binding, a user-declared error side always taking arm 0, `json.dec` on malformed input correctly discriminating because it returns NULL, and a bare `json.str` used as a `str` with no match working at all. Recorded in T-33 as inference from measurement, not as a claim about the compiler. If it holds, three issues are one fix, and it is the most valuable fix available in the language. |
| LS1.2 | M |  | **407 `string-concat-chain` → interpolation** — The token-efficiency win, and the one the catalogue measures most starkly. For a 3-part string: nested `concat` costs 20 proxy tokens, 95 minified bytes, 104 ms and 76,784 KB RSS; interpolation `"\(a)-\(b)-\(c)"` costs 13, 47, 82 ms and 39,136 KB. Roughly half the memory and a third fewer tokens, for the same output. 101 files. Prefer interpolation; `str.join` where the parts are already an array. |
| LS1.3 | S |  | **232 `unused-import`** — By hand, or by a script that understands type references. **Never with `toke --lint --fix`** until the upstream bug in F10.11 is fixed: its `unused-import` rule ignores `$type` references and deleted 197 needed imports here. Cross-check each removal against `$typename` usage in the same file before deleting. |
| LS1.4 | M |  | **The remaining 999 hints and warnings** — 974 `single-use-let`, 8 `loop-rebuilds-array`, 7 `unused-let`, 6 `mut-flag-if`, 3 `mutable-never-mutated`, 1 `empty-fn-body`. Lowest value per change and the largest diff, so it goes last and in reviewable batches by directory. `mut-flag-if` and `flag-soup` are pattern rules with measured verdicts behind them; the `single-use-let` hints are taste and may be declined with a reason. |
| LS1.5 | S |  | **Hold the line in the gate** — Add a lint step to `scripts/quality-gate.sh` that fails on any **error**-category rule (`discarded-value-result`, `unreachable-code`) and on `string-concat-chain`, with `scripts/known-defects.txt` as a shrinking allowlist and a story ID required per line. Hints stay advisory. Without this the sweep is a one-off and the count grows back. |

## Epic RV1: Re-verify the Done Markers That Need a Binary

*The build was red for four months. Roughly 120 stories were marked Done by reading code.*

> **The problem, stated plainly.** A story marked Done for behaviour that has never executed is worse
> than one marked not-started: it is an assertion with no evidence, and this document is the input to
> `claims.md`, which is the input to everything the project says publicly. Two examples already found and
> corrected show the shape — the X4a.6 preamble asserted *"All 172 modules compile, 19/19 tests pass"*
> when 58 of 199 compiled, and GA2.2 was Done for SQLCipher encryption the toolchain cannot do at all.
>
> Every row in scope gets exactly one of three outcomes. **Holds** — exercised and true, with the
> command that showed it. **Reopened** — exercised and false. **Untestable** — names what is missing.
> "Done" without one of those three is not an acceptable result for any row here.
>
> **Amended 2026-09-23, after the first pass at RV1.4 found markers wrong in BOTH directions.**
> Three Done rows are false — **GA1.2**, **GA1.4**, **GA1.8**, each now Reopened with the code that
> disproves it. One open row is done — **LS1.1a**, 11 of 13 landed. So a ledger that can only reopen is
> not a reconciliation, and RV1.1a is added for the inverse direction.
>
> Two further amendments the first pass forced:
>
> - **RV1.1 is not 65 rows of equal weight.** Only 26 of 122 test files import a production module,
>   reaching 21 distinct ones. Every T1–T14 row whose subject is outside those 21 is unverifiable *by
>   construction* — the verdict is "untestable", takes a minute, and needs no code reading. Partition
>   before working it, or RV1.1 reads as 65 code reviews when most of it is a join.
> - **X7 is the method, RV1 is the ledger, and the unit of work is one story row — not one test file.**
>   De-stubbing `tests/unit/privacy/test_placeholder.tk` *is* how GA1.2 gets re-verified. Scheduling
>   X7.2 and RV1.4 apart means reading the same module twice and writing two verdicts that can disagree.
>
> **RV1.7 could not start with counts, because the table schema was broken. RV1.7a fixed it
> 2026-09-23.** Recorded so the shape is not reintroduced: splitting story rows on `|` gave 551 rows
> with 6 fields, **38 with 5** — G1.1–G4.6 and AG1.1–AG1.8 used a 3-column table with **no status
> column at all**, carrying status as a trailing `**[Done]**` inside the description — and 5 with 7 or
> 8, read as unescaped `|` inside a cell. Status values were inconsistent in form too: 347 `**Done**`,
> 72 bare `Done`, 82 blank, plus `Blocked — …`, `Queued behind …`, `**Reopened**` and free prose. That
> is why two careful parses of this file disagreed on its own row count (598/477 against 594/485).
>
> The repair also corrected that survey. The 3-column tables were eight, not five — M1, M2 and X5 as
> well as G1–G4 and AG1, 40 rows — and only one row, AG1.7, had genuinely unescaped `|`; the other four
> were already written `\|` and it was the splitter that was wrong. Four rows had no closing `|` at all
> (F10.7, F10.11, RS1, RS2), which GFM renders anyway. The schema is now gated by
> [`scripts/check_backlog.py`](../scripts/check_backlog.py) (RV1.7b), and the counts in **SUMMARY** are
> generated from the rows.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| RV1.1 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23, all 65 rows carry a verdict | **The 65 T1–T14 rows** — Every one is marked Done under an epic preamble that demands runtime evidence: *"Tests verify runtime correctness — not just that files compile, but that functions return expected values for known inputs."* Several assert specific outcomes, e.g. T4.5 (*"gateway blocks RESTRICTED request when kill switch engaged"*), T10.1, T14.2 (*"POST to `/api/pipeline` with email in body returns anonymised prompt"*). Run them and record per-assertion results. Depends on the green build and on X7.<br><br>**Status detail — worked 2026-09-23.** All 65 rows now carry one of the three verdicts, and each verdict is written on its own row with the evidence. Result: **14 hold**, **51 reopened** — of which 37 cite a test file that is in `_archived-tests/` and therefore excluded from discovery, 12 cite a file that exists and imports nothing but `std.*`, and **2 were exercised against real code and found false** (T5.1, `core.storage.settings` binds the literal string `"now()"` into `updated_at`; T6.3, five assertions against `core.memory.decay`). The partition and its method are set out under **RV1.1 worked** above. `**Partial**` rather than Done because the 12 in bucket B become answerable as X7.2 de-stubs them, and each will then want a second look — this is the join, not the code review. |
| RV1.2 | M |  | **The X4a verification claims** — X4a.3 and X4a.4 (*"Verify loke/moke API handlers compile and execute"*), X4a.5.1–5.8 (each claims a named module now passes semantic checks — cheap to re-check against the current 141 failures), X4a.6.1–6.5 (live Ollama health, model list, `/api/pipeline` proxy) and X4a.7.1–7.4 (runtime CORS behaviour; X4a.7.4 is literally titled *"Verified implementation"*). X4a.4 is also the cited evidence for retiring BUG-7, so a failure reopens that too. |
| RV1.3 | M |  | **X4b.3–X4b.18 and T15.1–T15.10** — 13 test suites each claimed to compile independently and return 0, written under that epic's own blocker note that *"a standalone `--no-web-glue` compilation mode is needed in toke before any test binary can be produced"*. Plus the runner, the CI badge and `docs/test-coverage.md`'s assertion counts — F10.2 already established the badge was meaningless. T15.9 claims *"100% of existing tests passing"* and T15.10 *">80% of source modules have at least one test file"*. |
| RV1.4 | M |  | **The governance and privacy runtime claims** — GA1.1–GA1.4 and GA1.8 (pipeline, restoration, kill switch, audit logging, policy evaluation wired into the browser handler; GA1.5–GA1.7 and GA1.9 are already reopened), GA2.1 (keychain-only keys *"never written to disk, never appear in logs"*), GA2.4 (*"no PII appears in any log, console output, or error message"*), GA2.5 (binds `127.0.0.1` only), GA3.1–GA3.7 (ARIA, graduated warnings, cost estimate, cancel in-flight, thumbs-down), NC1.4–NC1.6 (schema actually sent, no fabricated values rendered, per-card provenance — BG1.1 and BG1.3 exist to re-verify exactly this). |
| RV1.5 | S |  | **The measurement claims** — F3.1 (*"10MB/s target"* regex detector), F3.9 (*"100% coverage"* privacy test harness, which AD1.8 records has never run because `run_tests.sh:25` skips it), F10.1 (the build refuses to link an incomplete build — confirm it fires at 141 failures rather than silently linking), F10.5 (*"type-check went 167 to 246 passing"*, re-measurable and contradicted by 58/199), X6.4 (the corrected coverage statement, honest only once tests run), VM1.5 (numbers out of CI, needs a CI run that gets past compilation). |
| RV1.6 | S |  | **F8.1–F8.4** — mDNS pairing, TLS 1.3 mutual auth with certificate pinning, remote model execution and the Exo integration, all four Done under an epic notice that simultaneously said they *"still need a compiler"*. TLS mutual auth with pinning is a security claim; it does not get to stay Done on inspection. |
| RV1.1a | S |  | **The inverse direction — open rows that are done** — RV1 as written only reopens Done rows. Confirmed cases: **LS1.1a** (11 of 13 landed), **GA5.1** and **GA5.2** (both fixed in `audit.tk`), **X8.5** (its hold cited NC1.9, which is Done), **MK21.3a** (`std.zip` exists now). Also **X4.2** claims delivery of a pre-push hook that `.git/hooks/` does not contain — a reopen in the other direction, found the same way |
| RV1.7a | S | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **Repair the table schema before anything is derived from it** — Every story row gets exactly 4 columns; the 38 rows in G1–G4 and AG1 gain a real status column instead of a `**[Done]**` buried in the description; no unescaped `\|` inside a cell; and status is drawn from a closed set — blank, `Spec done`, `Done`, `Reopened`, `⏸ On hold — <reason>`, `Blocked — <ID>`, `Queued behind <ID>`, `Partial — <reason>`. Rationale belongs in the description, not the status cell. **Nothing downstream is derivable until this holds**, which is why it is split out of RV1.7<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-23.** All 629 story rows now carry four cells and a closing `\|`. The three-column tables were G1, G2, G3, G4, AG1, M1, M2 and X5 — 40 rows, not the 38 estimated, because M1 and M2 were missed; 39 of the 40 carried `**[Done]**` and M1.7 did not, so it is now blank. AG1.7 was the one row with genuinely unescaped `\|` (`instant\|patient\|background`), which is why the earlier parse thought it carried no `[Done]` when it does. F10.7, F10.11, RS1 and RS2 had no closing `\|` at all. 47 status cells held rationale too long for the column; every one was moved verbatim into the Summary after `**Status detail:**`, and the transformation was checked to lose no word. `Spec done` is removed from the legend rather than kept on zero rows, and `Superseded by <ID>` is added because one row needed it. |
| RV1.7b | S | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **Generate the SUMMARY and gate the schema** — [`scripts/check_backlog.py`](../scripts/check_backlog.py), wired into `scripts/quality-gate.sh`. Seven checks: the four-cell schema and the closed status vocabulary; the SUMMARY regenerated from the rows between the `SUMMARY:BEGIN` and `SUMMARY:END` sentinel comments and diffed (`--fix` rewrites it); legend closure both ways; one live census per denominator per unit; a hold on a story that is already Done; cross-reference liveness including cycles; and error/module ladders, which must not fork. It does **not** judge whether a Done marker is true — that is RV1's human work, and a script claiming to do it would be the fourth thing in this repository that cannot fail. Proven able to fail against a deliberately broken copy. |
| RV1.7 | S |  | **Reconcile the document with itself** — The SUMMARY table and the story rows disagreed on the test suite (`53 \| 0` against 65 individually Done). Add a rule to the top of this document: the SUMMARY is derived, never hand-maintained, and a Done marker for runtime behaviour must name the command that exercised it. Consider a `scripts/check_backlog.py` that fails when the two disagree — the reconciliation script the retired `progress.json` was supposed to have and never got. |

> **Out of scope, deliberately:** F2.1–F2.9 (the throughput and tier claims) stay with epic **LC1**. They
> are blocked on Apple Silicon hardware, not on the compiler, and moving them here would imply the build
> can settle them.

### RV1.1 worked, 2026-09-23 — the partition, and why it is a join rather than 65 code reviews

The epic preamble demands runtime evidence. **Sixty-five** rows carry a file path in their own
description, so the first question about each is not "is this behaviour correct" but "does the file
this row cites exist, and does it exercise anything". That is a join against the tree, and it
settles fifty-one of the sixty-five without reading any production code.

One mechanical criterion per bucket, applied uniformly to all 65 so the sweep is auditable and
reversible:

| Bucket | Criterion | Rows | Verdict |
|---|---|--:|---|
| **A** | the named file is **not in the live tree** — it is in `_archived-tests/`, which `scripts/run_tests.sh` excludes by an explicit `-not -path '*/_archived-tests/*'` | **37** | **untestable** — no run has ever exercised it, in CI or locally |
| **B** | the named file exists and passes, and imports nothing but `std.*` — it declares its subject inside the test file | **0** | **untestable** — it cannot fail when the module changes |
| **C** | the named file exists and imports production code — so it was compiled, linked and run | **28** | **holds** (24) or **reopened** (4) |

Bucket A is the sharpest single fact in this reconciliation: **37 of the 65 Done markers cite a test
file that no runner can see.** Not a file that fails, not a file that was weakened — a file the
discovery glob removes before compilation. T8.1–T8.10 name `tests/unit/platform/*`, T10.1–T10.5 name
`tests/unit/governance_src/*`, T11.1–T11.4 name `tests/unit/mcp_broker/*` and
`tests/unit/memory_src/*`, T12.1–T12.3 name `tests/unit/feedback/*` and `tests/unit/agents/…`, and
T14.1 and T14.3 name integration and e2e files that were archived with them. Each is now
**Reopened** — and the reason is stated on the row itself, because "Reopened" without it would read
as an assertion having been disproved, which is not what happened. What is missing is the test, and
it is named.

Bucket B started the day at 26 and **ended at zero**, because X7.2 was worked at the same time and on
the same files — which is the reason the two epics are one piece of work. De-stubbing
`tests/unit/privacy/test_placeholder.tk` **is** how T1.2 gets re-verified; scheduling them apart
would mean reading the same module twice and writing two verdicts that can disagree. Every row whose
named file is in the live tree now cites a test that imports the module it is about, so bucket C is
the whole of the answerable set.

Bucket C is where the value is, and it is where both directions appear. Twenty-four rows **hold**, each
with the command that showed it written on the row. Four are **reopened on a failing assertion against
real code**, not on paperwork:

| Row | Test | What is false |
|---|---|---|
| **T2.5** | `tests/unit/router/test_intent.tk` | intent classification always answers `chat` with `method:"llm"` and confidence 0.72, whatever the prompt, because all three of its stdlib-result matches are error-arm-first. `router.tk:308` feeds that task into model selection on every request |
| **T3.3** | `tests/unit/optimiser/test_toon.tk` | `measuresavings` returns a bare `f64` across a module boundary and is corrupted (T-20) — the returned values are denormals whose bit patterns are `str.len` of the second argument |
| **T5.1** | `tests/unit/storage/test_settings.tk` | all four setters bind `"now()"` as a parameter, so `updated_at` stores the seven characters `n-o-w-(-)` |
| **T6.3** | `tests/unit/memory/test_decay.tk` | five assertions against `core.memory.decay`, including `apply` returning `-inf` at every age — and `sweeprows` deletes below a floor |

A fifth would have been `tests/unit/governance/test_scorecard.tk`: its `countquery` defect was measured
and reported, another worker fixed it in source during the same session, and the test is green against
the repair. It is named here because it is the clearest single demonstration of the epic's value.

**The moke and CLI rows are untestable for two different reasons, and the difference matters.**
`packages/moke/` fails to compile — 52 `COMPILE_FAIL` lines over 42 source files in
`build/trees/moke/break-report.txt`, including every module moke's 33 tests are named after. The CLI
tree, by contrast, builds **clean** (zero `COMPILE_FAIL`) and is invisible only because
`scripts/run_tests.sh` passes a single `-I build/interfaces` and links `packages/browser/build/ooke`
alone; a hand-built probe against `build/trees/loke-cli/interfaces` links and runs `cli.commands`
successfully. One is a code problem, the other is a one-line runner change. See
[test-coverage.md](test-coverage.md) for both measurements.
